ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U46	Hello, honourable Speaker of the Saeima! Colleagues! What is it? There are several draft laws, but I will only talk about this and my proposal, which is related to the restriction of gambling. I support the restriction of gambling in every case, but here are elements of creativity that have been created by the coalition and that will in fact have an impact on all the people of Latvia. It is about moving towards absolute censorship on the Internet, controlling citizens and gambling is just the beginning. And what is it? This concerns the first draft law “Amendments to the Law on Payment Services and Electronic Money”. Banks are obliged to report at the end of the year on those who try to participate in illegal gambling in some way, on the sites where they have been, and on those who have tried to use these services. The most interesting thing is, of course, that other laws contain appropriate penalties for those who try to use the services, but there is no penalty for banks at all if their money is transferred. I recommend that, in such a case, banks should report not citizens but should not be subject to full and absolute control by citizens, but banks should only report those unlicensed service providers that operate in Latvia and have tried to attract customers here. So, in fact, my proposal is focused on the existing coalition and Mr. Kariņš... You were born in America, with a lot of experience, an American citizen... I understand that you know something about freedom. Is it really necessary for Latvia to be the first country in the world to exercise total control over the recipients of services that operate freely on the Internet? The obligation should be imposed on others, not on us to restrict the freedoms of our citizens and to start to control them completely. So I recommend that I support my proposal and not reject it.

ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U191	Good morning, Madam President-in-Office of the Saeima. Mr. Prime Minister! The proposal was submitted by Ms Dana Reizniece-Ozola, Member. There is no support in the Commission.
ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U192	Let us start a debate. Name of Member Dana Reizniece-Ozola.
ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U193	Colleagues! The tyre management sector is also a large part of the shadow economy. This is due, to a large extent, to the fact that the price of tyre management has even decreased considerably now, compared to 2008, and the rate of natural resources tax has increased substantially. This actually leads to a failure to show or in principle not to accept the management of the tyre as it results in a financial disadvantage. The nature of our proposal implies that the tax paid on the importation of a vehicle into Latvia does not include the rate or tax on tyres. It should be paid separately. This will certainly ensure fairer competition and appropriate taxation in such a way that indeed used tyres are accepted and recycled. Yes, you can agree to a discussion at the meeting of the Commission about the fact that without this one, amendments to the Cabinet of Ministers' regulations are absolutely necessary, the procedure for administering this tax should be laid down, but we call for it to be done in support of this initial proposal at the same time. This proposal also has a positive fiscal impact. If it were possible to adopt regulation this year, 1.5 million is the amount that the public budget could have provided for the implementation of new measures. ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U416	Colleagues! The most significant support to non-governmental organisations from the state budget is provided through the support fund of non-governmental organisations administered by the Society Integration Fund. For this year, the draft budget currently amounts to EUR 770 000 and the support is planned in two lines of action. The first is strengthening the activities of NGOs, where organisations are supported when they operate in certain priority areas for at least the last three years. The second is to strengthen the protection of the interests of NGOs, which in fact support NGOs whose main activities are related to the protection of their interests at national level. Meeting with non-governmental organisations in the regions shows a significant lack of support from NGOs, i.e. a specific support programme for non-governmental organisations of regional and local interest. The essence of our proposal is to provide EUR 223 000 this year for a new call for projects and a new direction of NGO support, administered by the Society Integration Fund and aimed specifically at the support of non-governmental organisations of regional and local interest. Why is this important? We see that most of the applications in the previous competitions are very many, and most of them receive support from organizations from Riga, which are larger, which are more experienced, which, in most cases, also work on national issues. Regional and local NGOs, such as senior clubs, disabled societies, which organise the daily lives of people with disabilities, starting with sports games and ending with social support, active women's clubs, business support organisations working in regions and at regional level are simply unable to compete. We therefore believe that it would be useful to provide such support from the national budget without relying solely on foreign financial assistance, such as the Norwegian financial instrument or other funds, which these organisations are, of course, actively trying to use. We have also envisaged a source of funding, as in all other proposals where there is expenditure. We also offer these funds to ensure the abolition of excise duties on tobacco products used by diplomats. Overall, the resources for the NGO support fund are insufficient. We can see that only a quarter of all project applications submitted can already be supported. I therefore think that additional funding will be useful, particularly in a situation where corporate tax treatment has changed and there is substantial and broad support for entrepreneurs wishing to donate to non-governmental organisations. But the system is new, not all entrepreneurs are used to the new donation arrangements. Thus, especially this year, I think that funding would be necessary. Yes, thank you also to VARAM, who, in drawing up this year's draft budget, allocated additional funds to the NGO support fund.

He was also one of the signatories. Thank him. These amendments provide that a family caring for a child with disabilities, as well as people with Group I disabilities, receive the status of a protected user and pay less for electricity. Since the amount to which the special price for electricity applies is determined by the Cabinet of Ministers, I had repeatedly asked the Cabinet to set the maximum amount of support for this group, which was initially applied to large families, and it is 300 kilowatts per hour. But for incomprehensible reasons, the Cabinet of Ministers has set... has chosen to apply to this group the lowest possible amount of 100 kilowatts per hour corresponding to the status of low-incomed as protected users. I have received a number of requests from families and people with disabilities, from the Centre for the Protection of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities, to increase the amount of support, because the use of electricity for such protected users is justified by the use of specialised equipment and the adaptation of living space to human needs. The aid currently provided allows such users to save around... a little more than five euros per month from electricity. It's not much. Given that a total of up to 12 000 users have applied for this aid, we can conclude that this is very necessary for these users. And the request for more support is justified. By increasing support to 300 kilowatts per hour, this group can save up to 15 euros per month, which will already have a significant impact on the family budget. The country has pointed out many times that it cares especially for families of many children and children with special needs, and has pointed out this in various laws. However, for the most part, national policies towards specially protected families tend to provide more support than large families, and this is justified. For example, the same vehicle tax, which is payable at 50 per cent by large families, should not be paid at all by specially protected families. It would therefore be justified to respect consistency in the application of this support in the Cabinet of Ministers regulations as well. Families with children with disabilities who have received... Has applied for support for January 2019: there were approximately 2976 people. And people with Group I disabilities were 10 011 persons in January 2019. However, these figures are variable within the month and year. About January 2019, the amount of compensation for families with children with disabilities was slightly more than 16 thousand euros. And for January 2019 the compensation amount for Group I disabled was 52.5 thousand euros. Overall, the implementation of such measures will require around EUR million and EUR 700 000 per year from the national budget. But this support will be inextricable for those who need it most. Therefore, my fellow Members, I call on you to support this proposal, which determines the role of the Cabinet of Ministers to anticipate this support in the Cabinet of Ministers' regulations by increasing it. Thank you.

In this way, we have already significantly reduced the shadow economy in this area, which concerns fruit and vegetables, and the other sectors should be seen below. ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U575	Thank you. Colleagues! The budget not only allocates and transfers money but also instructs the executive authority to take financial policy measures. And that is my proposal. The majority of Latvian residents have no share capital, shares or securities. Their only wealth is housing, which guarantees the stability of their lives, their lives. They are the owners of two or three rooms apartments in apartment houses or the owners of small houses in the countryside. The real estate tax levied on their homes constitutes a significant part of the family budget. This is an integral part of the dwelling. There is no way to reduce this share or rent it in an optimal way, since housing is used for the sole purpose of living itself and it is not possible to leave it or use it for other purposes. On the other hand, the market offers several housing owners without risk to the family to rent areas that are not used for living, also to pledge real estate in the bank for lending (Sitting Manager knocks down with hammer.) whether to trade property in the real estate market, thus increasing its assets at the expense of the infrastructure offered by the free market. Real estate tax has a significantly smaller share in their family budget. In fact, this is an expression of social inequality. Such unfair distribution of free market opportunities is prevented by the application of the principle of progressivity in the taxation of immovable property. The demand for such a policy is obvious. I would remind you that two years ago, in the previous term, the Saeima received a collective application from 10 thousand Latvian citizens – an application “On the abolition of real estate tax on the only property”. The Saeima instructed the Cabinet of Ministers to make amendments to regulatory enactments in order to allow real estate tax not to apply to the only property or to apply the uniform reduced interest rate from the value of real estate. However, the Cabinet of Ministers did not support the abolition of real estate tax on the sole dwelling, instead encouraging to reduce the impact of cadastral value on the calculation of the tax. What's the end of that story? I find it difficult to say that I do not remember, but wrote that a political working group was set up that agreed to direct the Government's proposal to freeze cadastre values by 2020. Meanwhile, more than 50,000 signatures have already been collected for the only property in the Civil Initiative of ManaBalss.lv on the abolition of real estate tax. I propose to put an end to this initiative and, in the draft budget, to the government's task, which would fit well into the government's action plan. In 2014, I tabled a proposal which, in my view, would reduce social inequality more effectively and fairly than abolishing the tax on the sole property. And that is, the introduction of an exempt residential area with real estate tax. That year, the Working Party of the Committee on Budgets and Finance (tax) drew up a rule of the proposal, which stipulated that in cases where the municipality sets a higher rate of real estate tax than 0,2, 0,4 or 0,6 per cent of the cadastral value, a concession is applied when determining the part of the residential area exempt from real estate tax in the amount of 20 square metres per each person of Latvian citizens declared in this place of residence. So, for example, for a family with three persons, an apartment of 63 square metres would have to pay a tax on only three square metres. Five years ago, when government politicians still perceived tax progress as a revolutionary attack on the Saeima on mattresses, the proposal on the non-deserving minimum living space was also decided to stay in the drawer of the Chairman of the Budget and Financial (Financial) Commission. However, the times are changing, and for the new and younger generation of politicians I propose that the Cabinet of Ministers submit to the Saeima before 1 October this year the draft law “Amendments to the Law “On Real Estate Tax”” which establishes the part of residential area exempt from real estate tax for each person declared in this real estate and which enters into force on 1 January of the new year. Request to support.

There are few critical remarks about Latvia in the report. In particular, it quotes: "Even though the market income inequality is slightly higher than in other Member States of the European Union, the impact of tax and social transfers on reducing inequality is significantly lower than in other Member States. This reflects the low financing of social benefits and weak progress in income taxation." And then: “low progressivity makes its contribution to income inequality.” ( End quoted) The application of progressive tax in Latvia is inconsistent. If a company's income is distributed in dividends, it is taxed at 20 per cent, and this rate is not progressing, it is a flat rate. Both the owner of the cafe and the person on the Forbes list pay 20 percent of the tax. Thus, the principle of progressivity applies to salaries, but does not apply to dividends. And here's the answer, where to take money to fulfil the duties of the state before teachers and medical practitioners. Income tax dividends should also be subject to the principle of progressivity. The system must be reformed and start with the abolition of the solidarity tax. And here I have an ally. In order to ensure the full functioning of the inclusive education system, access for every child with special needs is necessary for a psychologist, a specialist educator, a logopedist and an assistant educator. Currently, the rates of these specialists in educational institutions are insufficient or not available at all for children with special needs, especially for those children for whom the Pedagogical Medical Commission has not recommended a Special Education Programme. Both education professionals and public organisations draw attention to the fact that children who do not have so-called codes at all but have special needs remain outside the system at present and do not receive support. Unfortunately, our pre-school institutions are not always prepared to receive and work fully with children who need special attention. As a result, it often turns out that a child has not learned to be social. Also, training at home does not bring good results. There is no small number of such children. Both highly gifted and those with mild mental and behavioural impairments but without diagnosis. Often schools do not know what to do with them, and they want to get rid of them. In order to reduce the number of problems faced by future pupils, it is necessary to ensure that all pre-school children have a similar opportunity to recognise their specific needs at an early age, as well as the conditions for early prevention of disorders. The main objective is to improve the health and development of the child. Supporting the proposal to provide additional funding in accordance with the Cabinet of Ministers’ Regulations “Regulations on the Provision of General Basic Education and General Secondary Education Institutions according to Special Needs of Educators” project, we will provide general education institutions with more independence and flexibility in supporting activities for children with special needs, and the result will be that children will be able to receive support and help to participate fully in society. Colleagues! Every child must have a value in society from an early age, so every child must have the same right to grow and educate in a safe and respectful environment where children are not threatened by humiliation, discrimination, violence or exclusion. Socially disadvantaged children deserve state support. I call for support for the proposal to provide the necessary funding of EUR 700 000 from unforeseen funds. Thank you.

In times of economic growth, these commitments are irresponsible, are they not, Prime Minister? Latvian science and innovation funding has been catastrophic for years. Despite continuous promises, this does not amount to more than 0.5 per cent of gross domestic product and has never experienced either fat years or economic growth. It is constantly at a time of crisis. The gap still remains, with the funding of Latvian science compared to the average of the European Union. On 6 February this year, representatives of the Ministry of Education and Science presented to the Sustainable Development Commission indicators demonstrating that Latvia has one of the lowest investments in science. And it's an obstacle to getting out of stagnation. The word ‘science’ is synonym with the word ‘innovation’. I know that some people do not understand such a simple sentence, at least three of them may not be understandable, and yet they do. The word ‘science’ is synonym with the word ‘innovation’. ParlaMint-LV_2019-04-03-PT13-530-U690	Dear colleagues, I would like to talk about both proposal 65 and proposal 66, because their substance is the same. I mean, that's what it's about. The Olympic centers make a significant contribution to the development of sport. Among them are the first Olympic Centre “Ventspils” founded in 1994. Having evaluated the contribution of the Olympic Centre “Ventspils”, the meeting of the Latvian National Sports Council on 19 October 2016 supported that Ventspils City Municipality will be granted funding for the implementation of the Olympic Centre “Ventspils” sports infrastructure development project, providing 1.55 million euros each year from 2017 to 2019. The financing for the development of sports infrastructure of state importance was also approved by the law on the medium-term budgetary framework for 2018, 2019 and 2020. In order to implement this joint development project, the Ministry of Education and Science has concluded an agreement with Ventspils City Council to grant EUR 1.55 million state funding for the implementation of the sports infrastructure development programme of the Olympic Centre “Ventspils” in January 29, this year, the Ministry of Education and Science, as well as the Ministry of the Government of Krišjānis Kariņš, not the Ministry of the previous Government. Based on these documents and in accordance with the principle of legitimate expectations, the Olympic Centre Ventspils has carried out the necessary organisational and technical work, as well as concluded contracts, and has received invoices that are, of course, not paid. State funding was intended not only for the development of a new swimming pool, rehabilitation centre technical project, but also for other important sports infrastructure. It should be stressed that the technical project development works of the swimming pool have already started last year and the agreement provides for the completion of design works and payments this year. In addition, the Latvian Swimming Federation also supports the construction of a swimming pool in the letter, as currently there is only one swimming pool of 50 metres in Latvia and it is located in Riga. One swimming pool is not enough to ensure the international level of competition to the extent necessary. Infrastructure improvement works have been started, but have not been completed in national sports facilities, I emphasize - in national sports bases, such as the BMX Summer stadium, as well as in the athletics and football stadium. Taking into account the state funding, in 2018 the reconstruction of the national basketball hall of the national sports base, which was built in 1997, started. In 2018, payments have been made for the partial reconstruction of athletic halls and dressing rooms to be completed this year. The state funding is also intended for expansion of the gym “Centrs” weightlifting hall and reconstruction of the sports hall floor. I would like to emphasise that Ventspils is currently the Latvian Weightlifting Centre, where, unfortunately, the currently existing Weightlifting Hall is not suitable for quality sports training. In 2018, research work on the weightlifting sports base was started and a sketch pre-project was developed. The funding is needed to improve the training conditions for the best weightlifters of the country - Rebeka Koha, Ritvar Suharev, Arthur Plēnsnieks and Vadim Koževņikov, who are preparing for the Olympic Games in Tokyo in 2020. This gym is also used by high-class athletes from other sports, who represent our country in global competitions, achieving the highest results. In preparation for the Olympics, as well as for the World and European Championships, training camps in the Athletic Hall of this hall are taken over by the Latvian bobsleigh team under the leadership of the coach Sanda Prūras. The municipality, relying on the principle that the laws adopted and legally concluded agreements must be complied with, is now in a situation where the State can afford not to execute the contract and the municipality will have to fulfil its obligations with the economic operators. The Ministry of Education and Science has allowed it to fail to comply with the terms of the cooperation agreement signed during the term of the new government on 29 January this year, which requires the municipality to inform the municipality at least one month in advance of the Ministry's plans to make changes to the financing arrangements by concluding a written agreement. No such agreement has been reached. Such an irresponsible, unsolved and illegal political decision not to award funding to projects already launched in national sports facilities will directly affect the entire sports sector and, in particular, high-class athletes. We will remember that 32 athletes represented our country in Rio de Janeiro during the previous Olympic Games in 2016, 10 or 30 percent of them were high-class athletes supported by Ventspils. And it was Mr. Rebeka Koha who showed the highest result in this Olympics, winning 4th place in weightlifting. It is unacceptable that, as a result of a change of political power, the country can fail to fulfil its commitments. The country must honour its commitments and provide funding. Especially because the Ministry of Education and Science is currently led by a party that stands up for an uncompromising rule of law. I call for support for the development of regional sport in the country as a whole, not on the basis of political conviction, sympathy and anti-pathy, which can lead to an irrational and ultimately damaging decision on the sport sector. I call for proposals 65 and 66 not to be supported. Thank you.
